ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION 
FROM AG AGRICULTURAL, LR LOW-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL, MR 
MEDIUM-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL, HI HEAVY INDUSTRIAL, AND CB 
COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO AG AGRICULTURAL AND LI LIGHT 
INDUSTRIAL FOR LAND LOCATED SOUTHWEST OF THE I-25 AND I-
80 INTERCHANGE ADJACENT TO ROUNDTOP RD." 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a 
zoning map amendment ("zone change") for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, 
Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That on March 4, 2024, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted 
a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zoning map amendment that is the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zoning map amendment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zoning map 
amendment that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of 
the UDC. 
 
 
Section 4.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
aforementioned application is hereby approved, and the zoning classification of the real property

Section 5.  That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the 
Official Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zoning map 
amendment as described in Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with 
Section 7 below. 
 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and 
publication, and upon the official filing of the affiliated The North Range Business Park final plat 
map with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

PUDC-24-18 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of 923.93 acres of land from LR, MR, CB, HI, and AG to AG and LI zone 
districts as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for 
approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
• 
UDC Article 5 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from LR, MR, CB, HI, and AG to LI and AG for 
approximately 923.93 acres of land. This property is proposed to be annexed and will be assigned the LR, MR, 
CB, HI, and AG zone based on the underlying County zone. The applicant has requests it be rezoned to AG 
and LI to allow further expansion of the North Range Business Park.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1)

PUDC-24-18 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. A companion application has been filed to bring this area into the Urban 
Services Boundary.   The applicant is working with the Board of Public Utilities to extend 
infrastructure to the area.   Upon approval of the related application, the property will be within 
the Urban Service Boundary.  Development will require infrastructure improvements and 
investment.   
 
The proposed zoning allows for expansion of an existing business park and allows for 
construction of a new data center. The proposed zone change is in accordance with the plans 
and policies outlined in PlanCheyenne and complies with review criteria 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The subject property proposed is located in an area with regional connectivity.   The location of 
North Range Business Park takes advantage of the location adjacent to Otto Road and the access 
to I-80 via Roundtop Road while maintaining a minimal impact on surrounding land uses. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criteria 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The property is proposed to be annexed into city limits and will be assigned the AG, LR, MR, CB, 
and HI zones. The applicant has requested to change the zone to AG and LI. The LI zone would 
allow for Limited industrial uses, which includes the proposed data center. The rest of the 
property would be zoned to AG which would be used as an interim holding zone.   
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The proposed area to be rezoned as LI is adjacent Roundtop Road and I-80 allowing sufficient 
access to the parcel. No changes to the network are proposed. The remaining area to be 
rezoned AG has no proposed development and the AG zone will act as a holding zone until a 
development is proposed.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The application supports the intent of the LI zoning district intent and applicability. The 
application falls on the edge of a Business Park as designated by the Comprehensive Plan and 
will serve the greater Cheyenne area by accommodating a variety of uses. The LI district is 

PUDC-24-18 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
intended for uses of very Light intensity which should be isolated and buffered in order to 
protect the community and to ensure unimpeded operation of the use. These uses do not fit 
into other general business or mixed-use areas due to the nature or intensity of operations. The 
LI district is applicable to larger land areas that are remote from other differing or incompatible 
land areas to minimize the potential for incompatible and negative impacts of uses in the 
district. The subject property must meet the applicable design requirements of the Unified 
Development Code.    
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criteria 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The subject property is well suited to meet all applicable standards of the LI district. The LI 
district allows a variety of uses and permits flexible development standards that will 
accommodate a variety of development proposals. Exterior building design is not regulated in 
the LI District.  
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criteria 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no review comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d) The zoning map 
amendment application is consistent with the zoning of surrounding properties and responds to current 

PUDC-24-18 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
market demands for the subject property. The zone change meets the review criteria of UDC 2.2.1.(d); 
therefore, staff recommends approval of the zoning map amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from LR, MR, CB, HI and AG to AG and LI. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Master Application 
4. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-03-05_TotalExtraction_39651471.pdf
 
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
The Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and City of Cheyenne Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
March 4, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Re-Appropriation to be considered for approval 
 
 
On March 11, 2024, the Governing Body will be referring a resolution to the Finance Committee 
to re-appropriate funds for the Fiscal Year (FY) 2024 City of Cheyenne Budget per W.S. § 16-4-
112 and 16-4-113.  Below is more detailed information regarding this re-appropriation request.  
 
GENERAL FUND 
1. Wage Increases.  In November 2023, salary adjustments were approved for 116 city 
employees through resolution #6367.  As I reported to you on December 1, 2023, four 
additional employees were identified that should have been included.  These employees 
had previously moved to a different job title, but their job duties did not change, and 
therefore their earlier city work experience should have been weighed more heavily.  Since 
that email was sent, Human Resources has identified an additional seven employees. This 
reappropriation will increase the applicable budget line items for the remaining six months 
of FY 2024 at a cost of $18,155 from General Fund reserves and $47,395 from all other 
funds. 
 
2. Repairs to the City’s Activity Center.   A request of $45,000 from General Fund reserves 
is being requested for Facilities Maintenance to make repairs to the City’s Activity Center, 
which include fixing the sewer line, replacing the hot water tank, and installing a new door. 
These repairs will allow Animal Control to move into the building. 
 
3. Spiker Parking Structure Security Services.  A request of $15,500 from General Fund 
reserves is being requested to hire professional security services at a cost of $3,100 for five 
months to be provided at the Spiker Parking structure. 
 
4. Thomas Heights Settlement.  The Governing Body approved a settlement on the Thomas 
Heights lawsuit agreeing that the city will complete drainage improvements under an 
established upon timeline.   An estimate to modify a nearby detention pond and install a 
sewer system is $350,000 to be paid from General Fund reserves. 

 
5. Cheyenne National Cemetery Road Improvements Project.  $4,500 is being requested 
from General Fund reserves to reimburse the City Engineer’s professional services account 
for geotechnical services provided for improvements to the road at the Cheyenne National 
Cemetery.  The remaining estimated project amount of $245,000 will also be paid from 
General Fund reserves. 
 
6. US Environmental Protection Agency (EPA) Climate Pollution Reduction Grant 
Application Assistance (CPRG).  The Mayor approved a contract with the Rocky 
Mountain Institute (RMI) to assist with a time sensitive grant application for the US EPA 
CPRG Program Implementation Grant, which is due April 1st.   $60,000 is being requested 
from General Fund reserves.   
 
7. Purchase of BEAST Facility.  The Governing Body approved the purchase of the BEAST 
facility in the amount of $4,259,075 ($4,250,000 was approved through Contract #7896, 
plus $9,075 in closing costs approved through Contract Modification #1).  General Fund 
reserves are being requested to cover this purchase. 
 
8. BEAST Facility Property/Liability Insurance.  $4,250 is being requested from General 
Fund reserves for property/liability insurance coverage of the new BEAST Facility. 
 
9. Axon Body Worn Video Cameras for Police Officers.  The Cheyenne Police Department 
received approval from the Governing Body to purchase 120 new body worn cameras for 
police officer staff.  The total equipment cost is $800,795, which is to be paid over a five-
year period.  The fiscal year 2024 payment is $160,160 and is being requested from General 
Fund reserves. 
 
10. New Boilers for the Municipal Building.  $240,292 is being requested from General Fund 
reserves to pay for the replacement of the Municipal Building boilers. The total cost to 
purchase the boilers was $215,292 and was conducted through an emergency procurement 
with Ameri-Tech.  An additional $25,000 is being requested to pay for professional 
contract services to hire a mechanical engineer to assist with proper installation of the new 
boilers.    
 
OTHER FUNDS  
The City of Cheyenne has received the following new grants and donations, or is reappropriating 
unspent existing grants, and are included on this reappropriation: 
Local and State Grant Fund (011) – new grants received: 
 $4,000 – Visit Cheyenne Marketing Grant 

 
Federal Grants Fund (025) – new grants received: 
 $1,696 – State Historic Preservation Office, Board & Staff Training: Colorado Saving 
Places Conference 
 $4,686 – State Historic Preservation Office, Board & Staff Training: National Alliance for 
Preservation Commissions Forum 
 $6,250 – Transportation Assistance Program, Safe Routes to School (grant award 
amendment) 
 $8,000 – State Historic Preservation Office, Cheyenne Airport Historic District 
Nomination (partial grant award appropriation) 
 $8,000 – State Historic Preservation Office, Cultural Resource Survey of African American 
Historical Sites/Buildings in Cheyenne (partial grant award appropriation) 
 $100,000 – Environmental Protection Agency, Climate Pollution Reduction Grants 
(CPRG) Program Implementation (partial grant award appropriation) 
 $125,720 – US Department of Energy, Energy Efficiency and Conservation Block Grant 
(EECBG) Program, for Civic Center lighting 
Other non-General Fund items being requested: 
1. Development Impact Fees Fund (013) – An amount of $700,000 is being requested for 
the potential land purchase of a new fire station 6 location.  This will be paid for by the 
public safety infrastructure fees held in this fund’s reserves. 
 
2. Recreation Programs Fund (014) – An amount of $20,000 is being requested to complete 
the Botanic Gardens Master Plan Update, which will be paid from miscellaneous 
donations.  An additional amount of $44,000 is being requested to purchase equipment for 
the new Gymnastics Facility.  The source of funds is donations from the Cheyenne 
Gymnastics Parent Booster Club. 
 
3. Miscellaneous Federal Grant Fund (025) – The Governing Body approved $2.5 million 
in American Rescue Plan Federal Recovery (ARPA) funds for construction of the City’s 
new gymnastics facility.  If costs exceed $2.5 million in the current fiscal year, additional 
funds will be reappropriated.   
 
4. Special Purpose Option Tax Fund (030) – An amount of $1 million dollars is requested 
to be paid from the West Edge District 2017 Ballot Special Purpose Option Tax Fund for 
City Contract #7782 with DHM Design Corporation for the Reed Avenue Corridor Project. 
 

5. Special Purpose Option Tax Fund (032) – An amount of $2,974,600 is requested to be 
paid from the Fire Trucks 2021 Ballot Special Purpose Option Tax Fund to pay for the 
Cheyenne Fire Rescue new fire apparatus. 
As always, if you have any questions, please give me a call at 637-6336 or email at 
rlockman@cheyennecity.org.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-03-05_TotalExtraction_63896694.pdf
 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Shrednik, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner  
 
Subject: A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR TO EXECUTE AN 
AMENDMENT TO THE DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT BETWEEN THE 
BOARD OF COUNTY COMMISSIONERS OF LARAMIE COUNTY, 
WYOMING, THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, AND COLL-IND, LLC 
REGARDING THE NIOBRARA ENERGY PARK." 
 
Date: 
February 6, 2024 
 
Background:  
This is an amendment to the original Development Agreement made between the Developer of 
Niobrara Energy Park, COLL-IND LLC and the City and County in 2012, Resolution 5295.  As 
described in this resolution and on page 3 of the original Development Agreement document, 
installation of an 8-foot sidewalk along the east side of Energy Drive to Fox Farm Road was 
required.  It is the opinion of staff that construction of this segment of sidewalk is not in the 
best interest of the City or County based on plans for future installation of storm sewer pipe in 
this area as any sidewalk installed will likely be removed and/or damaged in the future.  City 
staff, the County and the Developer have agreed that the Developer should  pay the City 
$60,000.00 toward construction of a Greenway connector alternative as shown in the attached 
exhibits. 
 
Enclosures: 
Resolution 
Niobrara Energy Park Development Agreement 5.7.2012 
Niobrara Energy Park Development Agreement Amendment #1 
Greenway Exhibit 
Niobrara Energy Park Exhibit 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR TO EXECUTE AN 
AMENDMENT TO THE DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT BETWEEN 
THE BOARD OF COUNTY COMMISSIONERS OF LARAMIE COUNTY, 
WYOMING, THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, AND COLL-IND, LLC 
REGARDING THE NIOBRARA ENERGY PARK." 
 
WHEREAS, On July 11, 2011, the Governing Body approved Resolution #5295 
authorizing the Mayor and the City Clerk to sign a County Final Plat and Development Agreement 
for the Niobrara Energy Park;  
 
WHEREAS, on May 7, 2012, Mayor Richard Kaysen signed the Development Agreement 
between COLL-IND, LLC ("Developer"), City of Cheyenne and the Board of Commissioners of 
Laramie County, Wyoming and the document was recorded in the Laramie County Clerk's office, 
reference # 590831, Bk 2274, page 343; 
 
WHEREAS, a master plan has been completed for the Fox Farm Road Corridor that may 
include certain storm water drainage improvements that may impact the Energy Drive right-of-
way planned improvements of the Development Agreement; 
 
WHEREAS, the original Development Agreement describes improvements to be installed 
by the developer to include an 8’ concrete sidewalk along the east side of Energy Drive. The area 
that this sidewalk is to be installed in appears to be the most likely corridor for a future storm sewer 
pipe from the Fox Farm road-way improvement project; 
 
WHEREAS, all parties agree that it would be cost effective to defer the installation of the 
sidewalk until after the Fox Farm Road project improvement has been completed; 
 
WHEREAS, all parties agree that the Fox Farm Road project timeline is uncertain and that 
any concrete poured today would likely be destroyed in the future; and, 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Developer has offered to pay the amount of sixty thousand dollars 
($60,000) to the City of Cheyenne toward the construction of an alternative Greenway connection 
as set forth in Exhibit A, attached, to be located within the right-of-way of east of Allison Road, 
south of ARP Elementary School and north of E. Allison Road to make an alternative transportation 
connection into the business park. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNNE, WYOMING, that the Mayor is hereby authorized and directed to execute the 
Amendment #1 to the Niobrara Energy Park Development Agreement and such other documents 
as necessary to effectuate the same. 
 
 
 
10:32 am, Mar 06 2024

 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
















 
NIOBRARA ENERGY PARK DEVELOPMENT AGREEMENT 
AMENDMENT #1 
 
This Development Agreement is made and entered into by and between Laramie County 
Wyoming, 309 W. 20th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (County), and COLL-IND LLC 
(Developer) for the purpose of defining specific improvements related to the development of the 
Niobrara Energy Park. 
 
WHEREAS this agreement will be an amendment to the Development Agreement 
executed between Coll-Ind, LLC, City of Cheyenne and Laramie County dated and recorded in 
the Laramie County Clerk’s office, reference # 590582, Blk 2273, page 1176; 
 
WHEREAS a master plan has been completed for the Fox Farm Road corridor that may 
include certain storm water drainage improvements that may impact Energy Drive ROW planned 
improvements from the above agreement; 
 
WHEREAS the current planned improvements to be installed by the developer include an 
8’ concrete sidewalk along the east side of Energy Drive. The area that this sidewalk is to be 
installed in appears to be the most likely corridor for a future storm sewer pipe from the Fox 
Farm road-way improvement project. 
 
WHEREAS both parties agree that it would be cost effective to defer the installation of 
the sidewalk until after the Fox Farm Road project improvement has been completed.  
WHEREAS both parties agree that the Fox Farm Road project timeline is uncertain and that any 
concrete poured today would likely be destroyed in the future. 
 
As such, the developer will pay the amount of $60,000.00to Laramie County or the City 
of Cheyenne toward the construction of an alternative Greenway connection, see exhibit attached, 
to be located within the right-of-way of E Allison Road, south of ARP Elementary School and 
north of E. Allison Road to make an alternative transportation connection into the business park. 
 
 
 
 
[THIS SPACE INTENTIONALLY LEFT BLANK] 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

This Development Agreement Amendment #1 was approved by the Laramie County 
Commissioners on  
day of  ______________, 2024.

(SEAL)

Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
COLL-IND, LLC BY: 
 
__________________________________________  
Ben Trautwein, Managing Member 

APPROX. 600 L.F.
10 FOOT WIDTH GREENWAY
Niobrara Energy Park Development Agreement
Proposed option to exchange 1300 l.f. Greenway along Energy Drive and provide funding for
600 l.f., 10-foot width Greenway connection north of Allison Draw and south of ARP Elementary
within E. Allison Road right-of-way.  $60,000.00.  Direct payment to the County or City.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND THE CITY 
CLERK TO SIGN A FINAL PLAT FOR NORTH ROW, CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (LOCATED AT 4612 VAN 
BUREN AVE)." 
 
 
WHEREAS, Brandon Swain, President of 1105 Ashford LLC, has subdivided said land in 
accordance with the statutes in such cases made and provided; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Brandon Swain, President of 1105 Ashford LLC, the owner of the above-
described land, has caused a subdivision plat of said land to be made, acknowledged, and certified, 
particularly describing the lots, blocks, easements, and rights-of-way; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above-described subdivision plat has been presented to the City of 
Cheyenne Planning Commission for consideration; the Planning Commission has recommended 
that said plat be approved by the Governing Body; and the plat has been duly executed by the 
Development Office. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the subdivision described as North Row, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed as presented, and that the Mayor 
and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and directed to execute said plat, 
when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor

FILE:
PUDC-24-4: North Row, Final Plat 
REQUEST:
A final plat creating 11 lots that include 10 single unit residential lots, and 1 lot 
for a commons space.
LOCATION:
Southeast corner of Dell Range Blvd and Van Buren Ave. Currently addressed 
4612 Van Buren Ave
APPLICANT:

2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-4  
 
 
March 4, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the North Row Final Plat with staff recommended 
conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat  
x 
UDC Article 3 Impact Studies 
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This is a request to final plat 10 single-unit residential lots and 1 lot used for common space, access, and 
drainage.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of final plat, the Commission must make 
the following findings required in UDC 2.1.3.d a detailed analysis is below. The purpose of a final plat 
review is to ensure compliance with the approved preliminary plat and its conditions of approval.   
 
1) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The layout of the plan is substantial with the approved preliminary plat laying out 10 single unit 
residential lots and 1 lot (lot 11) used for commons area, access, and drainage.  The proposed 
project is located at 4612 Van Buren Ave.  The project is bordered by Van Buren Ave to the east 
which will be the primary right of way.  No changes need to be made to the project property 
relating to the right of way streets.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
The preliminary plat was approved on March 3, 2023 subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The following exceptions to are granted: 
 a. Exception to UDC 4.3.3.c, regarding minimum block size and connectivity.  
2. Concurrent with final plat submittal:  
a. Submit a drainage study addressing drainage concerns for this development.  
b. Demonstrate correct utility provision.  
3. Correct the plat as follows:  
a. Increase the size of the townhome lots to meet minimum size requirements.  

PUDC-24-4  
 
 
March 4, 2024 
b. Remove the notary for City and have the Mayor and Clerk sign the plat.  
c. Specify ownership for the common lot. You may now proceed to the Final Plat 
process. Please fulfill the conditions above with the submittal of the Final Plat 
 
Drainage study- The submitted drainage narrative includes conformance with UDC 3.2.2b by 
including water quality improvements. This proposed conceptual drainage plan would meet UDC 
criteria for this site. The details of this proposed drainage plan can be worked out with the 
construction plan submittal on the project.   
 
Utility provision- The final utility layout may require easements to be added to the plat. The 
applicant is working with the utility companies to determine the placement of the easements. 
These easements will need to be shown on the plat prior to recording of the plat. 
 
 
Corrections to plat- All required corrections were made to the satisfaction of City staff.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion with conditions.   
 
3) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network 
 
Concepts regarding block layout, street design, and access were evaluated with the 
Preliminary Plat. The realignment of the plat brings blocks closer into compliance with the 
intent of the block size regulations, although the exceptions for topography, regional 
transportation routes, and mid-block trail corridors still need to be applied. 
 
Community Facilities and Fees 
 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
The Final Plat has slightly increased the non-exempt area. The required fees for the 0.36 
acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$140.00 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$140.00 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees  
$280.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure.  
 
Open Space Systems 
 
The open space will be provided with the site plan application for the cottage lot development 
per UDC Section 6.6.5. 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-4  
 
 
March 4, 2024 
Required Improvements and Engineering Specifications 
 
The final utility layout may require easements to be added to the plat. The applicant is working 
with the utility companies to determine the placement of the easements. These easements will 
need to be shown on the plat prior to recording of the plat. 
 
Public Safety Fee 
 
 
Public Safety Fees are required at $975.00 per dwelling unit. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public 
Safety Fees are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion with conditions.   
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Ownership common lot. 
2. Utility placement. 
3. Fulfillment of Preliminary Plat conditions. 
4. Block numbering. 
5. Clerical items.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.1.3.(d) for Final Plat 
approval. The Final Plat is consistent with the approved Preliminary Plat. Submittals to Staff have 
substantially addressed the conditions placed on the Preliminary Plat.   
 
If planning commission is okay with the conditions recommended by Staff, Staff would recommend 
approval. If the planning commission wants to see those specific easements, then Staff would 
recommend postponement of this item to the April 1, 2024 meeting. 
 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the project as submitted by the applicant. (Staff Recommendation) 
2. Recommend that the application be approved with specific conditions or modifications, and that 
a final plat addressing the conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing 
Body. 
3. Recommend that the application be denied based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-4  
March 4, 2024 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends that the Planning Commission recommend the Governing Body approve North Row 
Final Plat with the following conditions: 
1.
Prior to recording:
a)
Add utility easements.
b)
Add Block Number to the Plat.
Should the Planning Commission wish to see the easements prior to approval Staff would recommend 
postponement of this Final Plat to the April 1, 2024 meeting.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-03-05_TotalExtraction_34174343.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION REQUESTING THAT THE WYOMING STATE 
LEGISLATURE CONSIDER LEGISLATION AUTHORIZING THE SALE 
OF ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGES WITH AN ALCOHOL BY VOLUME OF 
5% 
AT 
GROCERY 
STORES 
AND 
CONVENIENCE 
STORES 
THROUGHOUT THE STATE." 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body recognizes the need for improving the convenience of 
life for existing state residents, prospective new residents, and visitors alike;  
 
WHEREAS, one avenue for making daily life more efficient and convenient is to allow 
Wyoming residents and visitors to this great state to purchase alcoholic beverages with an alcohol 
by volume (ABV) of 5% at grocery stores and convenience stores, which stores can be easier to 
locate and more convenient to patronize than retail liquor stores; 
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body believes that the State's current statutory structure 
governing the sale of alcoholic beverages creates confusion on the part of Wyoming residents who 
cannot comprehend why alcoholic beverages may be sold at certain grocery stores and 
convenience stores in the state, but not at all of them; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body further believes that the current statutory structure 
governing the sale of alcoholic beverages is stifling economic growth in both larger and smaller 
communities because the number of liquor licenses that may be issued is tied to arbitrary 
population formulas specified by state statute; 
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body recognizes the value and necessity of local control 
regarding the issuance of liquor licenses by cities and towns, which are also charged with funding 
and administering emergency services and law enforcement;  
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body recognizes and appreciates the entrepreneurial spirit of 
the businesses who apply to the City of Cheyenne seeking a liquor license. However, the current 
statutory structure illogically constricts alcoholic beverage sales, placing governing bodies across 
the state in a precarious situation of picking “winners and losers” amongst a variety of qualified 
candidates. The Governing Body believes this is detrimental and antithetical to a free market 
economy; and 
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body has requested that the Wyoming Association of 
Municipalities draft a resolution on this topic similar to the one herein, which resolution, among 
other things, would also express support for grocery stores and convenience stores being able to 
sell alcoholic beverages with an ABV of 5%. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body hereby requests that the Wyoming State 
Legislature, and the appropriate legislative committees, review, consider, and adopt modifications 
to the current statutory structure governing the sale of alcoholic beverages in the state's city and 
1:04 pm, Feb 05, 2024

Page 2 of 2 
towns to allow alcoholic beverages with an ABV of 5% to be sold in grocery stores and 
convenience stores. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---

FILE:

REQUEST:

PUDC-24-7 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Cheyenne Terrace, 2nd Filing Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to 
the Governing Body with staff recommended conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
x 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for roughly 8.87 acres of land. The subject property 
is developed at the north end with multi-family style development. The proposed plat would subdivide the 
parcel into 76 lot consisting of 74 cottage lots, 1 common lot and a lot for the existing development. The 
proposed plat is utilizing the Cottage Lot section of the Unified Development Code (6.6.5). There is a 
concurrent zone change with this platting action proposing to change the zoning of the proposed cottage lots 
to NR-2, Neighborhood Residential – Medium Density. The zone change is PUDC-24-17.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land and surrounding area has 
been designated as Urban Residential. The Urban Residential designation encourages a range of 
more intensive residential uses, although the intent is not to change existing stable 
neighborhoods. The existing neighborhood has a mix of housing types and densities, and the 
proposed development fits within the range of the existing neighborhood, while providing a 
different housing option.

PUDC-24-7 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne supports the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision 
of services (including emergency and education services). This development is proposed in an 
area which has largely existing infrastructure and limited expansion of infrastructure is 
necessary. 
  
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed development arrangement is proposing to utilize a Cottage Lot style of 
development. Cottage Lots are considered an effective way to utilize infill parcels and are useful 
as an alternative form of housing. The development of this site with a Cottage Lot style of 
development would not adversely affect the surrounding existing development patterns. The 
proposed development also creates a connection between Patton Avenue and Snyder Avenue.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$1,610 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$1,610 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$3,220 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community which has been previously developed. 
There is limited opportunity to modify the block layout or street network. The proposed plat 
does provide a private access road through the development which connects Patton Avenue 
and Snyder Avenue.  
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is required at 750sqft per unit or 8% of the gross development area, whichever 
is less. Lots 2, Block 1 is at least partially proposed as an open space parcel. Based on the 

PUDC-24-7 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
development exhibit proposed with this application, the anticipated open space included 
with this development will exceed the required minimum. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. Easements are provided for BOPU utilities and for other utilities. Some lots 
will need service lines clarified, but as a Cottage Lot development, a site plan will be 
required, and service provision may be able to be addressed with the site plan or 
engineering plan. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any residential building on any 
of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the issuance 
of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the study provided. 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the study provided. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed application occurs in concert with a proposal to zone the land NR-2 Neighborhood 
Residential – Medium Density. The NR-2 zone is intended for a mix of medium-density 
residential uses in a compact development pattern (with smaller, walkable blocks). The NR-2 
zone allows for Cottage Lot development – a scenario in which multiple single-unit lots have 
access to a common open area.  

PUDC-24-7 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
 
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
Lot 2 is proposed to be a common lot for lots 3-74 and will be maintained by the owners of 
those lots. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-7 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends minor 
corrections as conditions to the plat. These corrections should be resolved prior to the final plat 
being recorded.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections. 
2. Utility and easement correction. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant with conditions.  
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-7 
 
 
March 4, 2024 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following conditions: 
1. Prior recording: 
a. Remove reference to right-of-way or ROW for the private access easement. 
b. Add a 20-foot BOPU easement between Lots 33-38 and 45-51 extending from the BOPU 
easement in Calton Avenue to the north property line of Lot 2.  
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-03-05_TotalExtraction_31518349.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION REQUIRING THE STUDY AND SUBMITTAL OF A 
REPORT TO THE GOVERNING BODY ANALYZING THE BEST 
AVENUE FOR CORRECTING DEFICIENCIES AND IMPROVING AND 
MAINTAINING THE WEST ENTRYWAY TO THE MUNICIPAL 
BUILDING TO ENHANCE PUBLIC PERCEPTION AND EXPERIENCE." 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne's municipal building is located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue 
and originally opened in 1979; 
 
WHEREAS, the municipal building serves eleven (11) City departments and 
approximately ninety-one (91) City employees, and also serves as the gateway for the general 
public to interact with the city government; 
 
WHEREAS, the public's initial impression of the municipal building is important. 
However, the exterior conditions of the municipal building, and building grounds in general, are 
not ideal and while routine maintenance and upgrades have occurred, additional work remains; 
 
WHEREAS, the west entryway to the municipal building serves as the main access-point 
for all community patrons and is the first element that community patrons encounter when arriving 
at the municipal building. As the first element that community patrons encounter, it is important 
that the entry be inviting so as to leave a positive impression with the visiting public; 
 
WHEREAS, currently the main entrance to the municipal building requires additional 
improvements, with issues where soffits are rusted. The exterior brick is also failing, cracking 
vertically and breaking away from the existing structure. In addition, efflorescence is present on 
areas of the exterior brick, which detracts from the appearance of the municipal building; 
 
WHEREAS, portions of the exterior pavement are also failing around the main entrance, 
notably through the deterioration of the pebble walkway, which failures present tripping hazards, 
further detracting from the appearance of the building; 
 
WHEREAS, ingress and egress from the main entrance also suffers from deficiencies due 
to the age and current state of the doors at the entryway. These doors are often difficult to open 
and close and the security measures tied to the entryway can malfunction, which impairs the 
security of the building;  
 
WHEREAS, the exterior landscaping around the main entrance has also not been 
adequately and consistently maintained, with shrubs and trees requiring trimming and planters 
needing to be cleared of debris, which issues also detract from the public's impression of the 
municipal building; 
 
WHEREAS, beyond the aesthetic concerns surrounding the main entrance to the municipal 
building, there are also areas where the City is not in compliance with its own design standards 
and requirements, including failures specific to exterior materiality, lighting, and signage 
requirements as specified by the City's Unified Development Code; and 
9:09 am, Mar 19, 2024

Page 2 of 2 
 
 WHEREAS, ensuring the main entrance is well maintained and serves as an inviting 
gateway for the general public is important and must be a priority of the City. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Public Works Department begin analyzing the process for 
improving the negative condition of the main entrance to the municipal building, and study the 
attendant costs for making such improvements; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Public Works Department report to the Governing 
Body not later than sixty (60) days after the effective date of this resolution detailing the results of 
the analysis and recommended steps for improving the conditions of the main entrance to the 
municipal building and for enacting a continuing maintenance plan to ensure the main entrance 
sustains a positive public impression; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that as part of the report submitted to the Governing Body, 
the Public Works Department shall prioritize and make recommendations on the order in which 
the City should address the deficiencies identified herein, and any other issues surrounding the 
main entrance to the municipal building, and specify the associated costs for completing the 
improvements;  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that as part of the report submitted to the Governing Body, 
the Public Works Department shall also detail possible funding sources for completing such work 
and make recommendations on whether the work should be completed internally using City staff 
and resources or completed through third-party contract services; and 
 
BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that upon submittal of the report, the Governing Body shall 
schedule a work session during which appropriate staff from the Public Works Department shall 
present the results of the study, the substance of the report and associated recommendations. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-03-05_TotalExtraction_36316300.pdf
Page 1 of 10 
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
 
between 
MEMORIAL HOSPITAL OF LARAMIE COUNTY D/B/A CHEYENNE REGIONAL 
MEDICAL CENTER, 
VOLUNTEERS OF AMERICA, 
HEALTHWORKS, 
COMMUNITY ACTION OF LARAMIE COUNTY, 
WYOMING DEPARTMENT OF CORRECTIONS, 
CHEYENNE POLICE DEPARTMENT, 
LARAMIE COUNTY SHERIFF’S OFFICE, 
LARAMIE COUNTY DISTRICT ATTORNEY, 
LARAMIE COUNTY DRUG COURT, 
LARAMIE COUNTY TREATMENT COURT PROGRAMS, 
OFFICE OF THE STATE PUBLIC DEFENDER, 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, AND 
LARAMIE COUNTY 
 
Regarding 
LARAMIE COUNTY LAW ENFORCEMENT ASSISTED DIVERSION (LEAD) 
PROGRAM 
POLICY COORDINATING GROUP: OPERATIONS, GOVERNANCE, AND 
RESPONSIBILITIES 
 
WHEREAS, the parties to this Memorandum of Understanding (“MOU”) recognize the 
following: Defendants that are arrested sometimes exhibit signs of an underlying mental illness or 
substance use disorder that contributed to the alleged crime for which the defendant has been 
arrested. Defendants with mental illness or substance use disorders are often experiencing chronic 
homelessness which further exacerbates their behavior to commit low-level offenses. Such 
defendants are charged with low-level, non-violent, drug-related offenses and are detained in the 
county jail for extended periods of time. Our current justice system does not allow for such 
defendants with low-level offenses to receive treatment in connection to their criminal charge and 
are shortly re-arrested on similar charges. To reduce recidivism, a community-based MOU is 
crucial to divert from the criminal justice system to proper community mental health facilities, 
treatment, and recovery resources. The long-term goal of diversion is to help defendants break the 
cycle of repeated contact with the justice system by being guided toward a path of sustainable 
wellness through support services; and 
 
WHEREAS, the arresting, booking, processing, and jailing of defendants committing 
offenses related to mental illness, drug involvement, chronic homelessness, poverty, and other 
health and wellness issues has had limited effectiveness in improving either public safety or public 
order. LEAD Laramie County will build on existing efforts to address the needs of repeat drug 
offenders through an initiative based on shared planning, decision-making, data sharing, and 
evaluation that will 1) expand outreach to eligible individuals within the county, 2) enhance 
coordination of service delivery through the existing social service network, 3) increase referrals 
to substance use and mental health treatment centers; and 4) improve collaboration and 
8:27 am, Mar 06 2024

Page 2 of 10 
communication among law enforcement agencies and social services providers.  From the 
perspective of the Sequential Intercept Model, LEAD intercepts the individual and diverts the 
behavioral problem at the point of law enforcement response (Intercept 1), to channel drug-
involved individuals into a community-based intervention whenever possible and appropriate. 
 
NOW THEREFORE, the Parties state their intentions as follows: 
 
A. 
Formation and Purposes of the Laramie County LEAD Policy Coordinating Group 
(PCG).  A Policy Coordinating Group is hereby formed for the LEAD pre-arrest/booking 
diversion program. The purposes of the PCG are to review and provide feedback on the 
Referral and Diversion Protocols for LEAD participants, make criminal justice and human 
services data available for comparison and evaluative purposes, and provide policy 
guidance and administrative oversight for the LEAD program’s operations and evaluation.  
 
1. 
LEAD Policy. The Laramie County Sheriff's Office (LCSO) will assume the 
central role in managing the Policy Work Group. The Policy Work Group will 
provide the framework for the LEAD program. In this capacity, it will collaborate 
with the following entities in shaping and refining the program's policies: 1. 
Cheyenne Regional Medical Center (CRMC); and, Cheyenne Police Department 
(CPD).  Further consultations with additional organizations may be considered 
upon receiving approval from the LCSO. 
 
B. 
Purpose. The purpose of this MOU is to outline the roles and responsibilities of the 
involved parties as it pertains to the Laramie County LEAD Program, with the goal of 
improving public safety and reducing recidivism in low-level nonviolent offenses. 
 
C. 
Responsibilities of Policy Work Group. The role of the Policy Work Group is to make 
policy-level decisions regarding the LEAD program and to provide periodic administrative 
oversight of the program. Specific responsibilities include, but are not limited to, the 
following:  
 
1. 
LCSO 
 
The LCSO has been designated as the lead agency for LEAD Laramie County and 
will oversee project coordination and grant administration in coordination with the 
Laramie County Grants Office and will be an active participant in ongoing planning 
and discussions. The LCSO will ensure appropriate implementation of LEAD by 
providing deputy training on referral criteria and protocol; participate in the 
development of LEAD training and operational and data sharing protocol; and serve 
on the Operational Work Group Committee. LCSO will provide 3 staff positions 
for the LEAD program. The LCSO will hire and supervise one full-time Project 
Manager and two full-time Case Managers. The Project Manager will be 
responsible for the administrative operations of LEAD. The Case Managers will be 
responsible for the planning and implementation of LEAD, to include 
comprehensive case management services. 
 

Page 3 of 10 
2. 
CPD 
 
The CPD is committed to participate in LEAD on both a policy and operational 
level. CPD will ensure appropriate implementation of LEAD by CPD officers; 
provide officer training on referral criteria and protocol; participate in the 
development of LEAD training and operational and data sharing protocol; and serve 
on the Operational Work Group Committee. 
 
3. 
CRMC 
 
CRMC will receive referrals from LEAD staff and deliver medical, mental health, 
and substance use treatment services to LEAD participants. CRMC agrees to 
participate in information sharing and will serve on the Policy Coordinating Group 
and Operational Work Group Committee. CRMC will collaborate and cooperate 
with all parties included in this MOU to facilitate positive outcomes for mutual 
participants.  
 
D. 
Responsibilities of Operational Work Group Committee.  The Operational Work Group 
Committee is comprised of direct service providers that play a crucial role in providing 
strategic guidance and facilitating communication between the project team and various 
stakeholder groups, contributing to the initiative's overall success. It serves as an advisory 
body, offering diverse perspectives and input to inform decision-making. Their primary 
responsibilities include: 
 
1. 
City of Cheyenne 
 
The City of Cheyenne will be an active participant in ongoing planning and 
discussions and serve on the Operational Work Group Committee with the CPD. 
 
2. 
Laramie County, Wyoming 
 
The governing body of Laramie County will disburse grant funds in accordance 
with the grant application and will maintain responsibility for all reporting, 
reimbursements and grant administration.  
 
3. 
Laramie County District Attorney’s Office 
 
The Laramie County District Attorney’s office will be an active participant in 
ongoing planning and discussions and serve on the Operational Work Group 
Committee.  The District Attorney’s office can also refer individuals to the 
program. 
 
4. 
Office of the State Public Defenders (Laramie County Division) 
 
The Laramie County division of the Office of the State Public Defender may make 
referrals to the LEAD program if they believe the individual meets eligibility 

Page 4 of 10 
criteria and could benefit from additional support in the community. The Public 
Defender’s Office agrees to participate in information sharing and serve on the 
Operational Work Group Committee.   
 
5. 
Laramie County Treatment Court Programs 
 
The Laramie County Treatment Court Programs may make referrals to the LEAD 
program if they believe the individual meets eligibility criteria and could benefit 
from additional support in the community. The Treatment Court Programs agree to 
participate in information sharing and serve on the Operational Work Group 
Committee.  The Treatment Court Programs will collaborate and cooperate with all 
parties included in this MOU to facilitate positive outcomes for mutual 
participants.   
 
6. 
Volunteers of America 
 
Volunteers of America (VOA) will receive referrals and deliver mental health and 
substance use treatment services to LEAD participants. VOA agrees to participate 
in information sharing and will serve on the Operational Work Group Committee. 
VOA will collaborate and cooperate with all parties included in this MOU to 
facilitate effective patient treatment prior to, during, and after any emergency 
detention or involuntary hospitalization. 
 
7. 
HealthWorks 
 
HealthWorks will receive referrals from LEAD staff and deliver medical, mental 
health, and substance use treatment services to LEAD participants and agree to 
participate in information sharing and serve on the Operational Work Group 
Committee. HealthWorks will collaborate and cooperate with all parties included 
in this MOU to facilitate positive outcomes for mutual participants.  
 
8. 
Community Action of Laramie County 
 
Community Action of Laramie County (CALC), specifically Crossroads 
Healthcare Clinic, will receive referrals from LEAD staff and deliver medical, 
mental health, and substance use treatment services to LEAD participants. CALC 
agrees to participate in information sharing and serve on the Operational Work 
Group Committee. CALC will collaborate and cooperate with all parties included 
in this MOU to facilitate positive outcomes for mutual participants.  
 
9. 
Wyoming Department of Corrections (Laramie County Field Office) 
 
The Laramie County field office of the Wyoming Department of Corrections 
(DOC) may make referrals to the LEAD program if they believe the individual 
meets eligibility criteria and could benefit from additional support in the 
community. DOC agrees to participate in information sharing and serve on the 

Page 5 of 10 
Operational Work Group. DOC will collaborate and cooperate with all parties 
included in this MOU to facilitate positive outcomes for mutual participants. 
 
E. 
Confidentiality. 
 
1. 
The parties and their employees and affiliates shall maintain the confidentiality of 
all patients and/or individual party information in accordance with all applicable 
state and federal laws and regulations regarding the confidentiality of such 
information. The parties shall not divulge such confidential information to any third 
parties without the patient’s or party’s prior written consent, except, as to patients, 
unless required by law or as necessary to treat such patient. CRMC shall not 
disclose protected health information to the parties under this agreement unless a 
valid HIPAA release has been signed by the patient authorizing the disclosure. 
Similarly, the other parties shall not request protected health information unless a 
valid HIPAA release has been signed by the patient.  
 
2. 
The parties shall comply with all requirements established by HIPAA regarding 
safeguarding and protecting individually identifiable health information from 
unauthorized disclosure, including any patient’s individually identifiable health 
information. The parties agree to amend this MOU as required to comply with 
HIPAA, and to protect any individually identifiable health information that any 
party accesses performing under this MOU. The parties shall ensure that their 
respective employees, agents, and contractors are aware of and shall comply with 
the aforementioned obligations. 
 
F.  
General Provisions. 
 
1. 
Amendments. Any party may request changes to this MOU.  Any changes, 
modifications, revisions or amendments to this MOU which are mutually agreed 
upon by and between the parties to this MOU shall be incorporated by written 
instrument, and effective when executed and signed by all parties to this MOU. 
 
2. 
Third Parties.  The parties do not intend to create in any other individual or entity 
the status of third-party beneficiary, and this MOU shall not be construed so as to 
create such status.  The rights, duties and obligations contained in this MOU shall 
operate only between the parties to the MOU and shall ensure solely to the benefit 
of the parties to this MOU. 
 
3. 
Applicable Law and Venue. The Parties mutually understand and agree this MOU 
shall be governed by and interpreted pursuant to the laws of the State of Wyoming.  
If any dispute arises between Parties from or concerning this MOU or the subject 
matter hereof, any suit or proceeding law or in equity shall be brought in the District 
Court of the State of Wyoming, First Judicial District, sitting in Cheyenne, 
Wyoming. The foregoing provisions of this paragraph are agreed by the Parties to 
be a material inducement in executing this MOU.  This provision is not intended, 

Page 6 of 10 
nor shall it be construed to waive CRMC’s, LCSD’s, CPD’s, City of Cheyenne and 
Laramie County’s governmental immunity as provided in this MOU. 
 
4. 
Entirety of MOU. This MOU, consisting of ten (10) pages, represents the entire 
and integrated MOU between the parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, 
representations and agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
5. 
Invalidity. If any provision of this MOU is held invalid or unenforceable by any 
court of competent jurisdiction, or if any Party is advised of any such actual or 
potential invalidity or inability to enforce, such holding, or advice shall not 
invalidate or render unenforceable any other provision hereof.  It is the express 
intent of the Parties that the provisions of this MOU are fully servable.  
 
6. 
Discrimination. All Parties agree they will not discriminate against any person 
who performs work under the terms and conditions of this MOU because of race, 
color, gender, creed, handicapping condition, or national origin.  
 
7. 
ADA Compliance. All Parties agree they will not discriminate against a qualified 
individual with disability, pursuant to a law as set forth in the Americans with 
Disabilities Act, P.L. 101-336, 42 U.S.C § 12101, et seq., as amended, and/or any 
properly promulgated rules and regulations relating thereto. 
 
8. 
Governmental/Sovereign Immunity.  The City of Cheyenne, Laramie County and 
CRMC do not waive their governmental immunity by entering this MOU, and fully 
retain all immunities and defenses provided by law with respect to any action based 
on or occurring as a result of this MOU.  
 
9. 
Indemnification.  Each Party to this MOU hereby acknowledges and agrees that it 
shall be responsible for its own acts arising out of this MOU.  No Party agrees to 
defend or indemnify another Party. 
 
10. 
Force Majeure. No Party shall be liable to perform under this MOU if such failure 
arises out of causes beyond control, and without the fault or the negligence of said 
Party.  Such causes may include, but are not limited to, Acts of God or the public 
enemy, fires, floods, epidemics, pandemics, quarantine restrictions, freight 
embargos, and unusually severe weather.  In any case, however, a failure to perform 
must be beyond the control and without the fault or the negligence of said Party.  
 
11. 
Term of MOU. This MOU is effective upon the day and date last signed and 
executed by the duly authorized representatives of the parties to this MOU and the 
governing bodies of the parties’ respective counties or municipalities and shall 
remain in full force and effect until a reevaluation which will be performed every 
3 years.  This MOU may be terminated, without cause, by any party upon thirty 
(30) days written notice, which notice shall be delivered by hand or by certified 
mail. 
 

Page 7 of 10 
12. 
Payment. No payment shall be made to any Party by any other Party at a result of 
this MOU. 
 
13. 
Severability. Should any portion of this MOU be judicially determined to be illegal 
or unenforceable, the remainder of the MOU shall continue in full force and effect, 
and either party may renegotiate the terms affected by the severance. 
 
14. 
Certification Regarding Status with Government Health Programs. LCSD, 
CPD, Laramie County, and City of Cheyenne and CRMC certify individually that 
it, and its employees and/or agents (“Related Persons”) (a) are not now debarred 
and have never been, excluded or otherwise ineligible for participation in any 
government health care program(s); (b) have not been convicted of a felony offense 
in the immediately preceding seven (7) years; and (c) are not now subject to, and 
have no reason to believe that they are subject to, any specific investigation for 
violation of federal, state, or local criminal or civil law or regulation.  Any Party 
shall report in writing to the other Parties immediately if it becomes aware of such 
action, investigation, or effort to debar or exclude it, or any related person, from 
any government health care program.  Failure to disclose any relevant information 
regarding these matters is grounds for immediate termination of this MOU with 
case at the sole discretion of the other Parties.  
 
15. 
Signatures. In witness whereof, the parties to this MOU through their duly 
authorized representatives have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out 
below, and certify that they have read, understood, and agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this MOU as set forth herein. 
 
 
The remainder of this page intentionally left blank.
