C. Authorized. When the police chief finds a vehicle parked in violation of the provisions of 
this code, such person may immobilize the vehicle or cause the same to be done if that 
vehicle appears on the scofflaw list. 
 
D. F. 
Alternate Procedure. The provisions of this This section are declared is intended to 
provide an alternative manner for the enforcement of the provisions of enforcement 
authority under this code pertaining to parking violations and shall not preclude 
impoundment of the a vehicle parked as otherwise authorized under Section 10.12.160 of 
the city code this chapter or any other method of enforcing the parking ordinances. 
 
E. G. 
Penalty. Any person found guilty of tampering who tampers with an immobilization 
device in an attempt to remove or destroy it, the device is subject to the general penalty 
provision under guilty of a misdemeanor and punishable as provided by Section 1.24.010 
of the city code. 
 
F. H. 
Post-Immobilization Post-Deprivation Review. The registered owner of any 
vehicle owner immobilized or impounded under this section may seek a post-deprivation 
review of the immobilization by submitting a written request to the police department 
parking administration manager within ten (10) days of the placement of the 

Page 5 of 6 
 
immobilization device and notice on the vehicle, as established by the date of the 
immobilization notice date. The police department required under subsection (C) of this 
section. The registered owner of any vehicle towed and impounded as authorized under 
this section may seek a post-deprivation review by submitting a written request to the 
police department parking administration manager within ten (10) days of date of the 
vehicle being towed. The request shall include an explanation by the owner of why the 
immobilization or tow was allegedly improper.  
 
I. Upon receiving the request for a post-deprivation review as provided under subsection (H) 
of this section, the parking administration manager shall provide the request for review to 
the municipal court judge. Upon timely receipt of such written request, the municipal court 
judge shall, within a reasonable time as established by the court, conduct a review on the 
issue of whether the immobilization was proper and shall issue a written decision setting 
forth the reasons on which the decision is based, provided, however, that any previously 
adjudicated parking infractions that formed the basis of the vehicle's scofflaw status shall 
not be subject to the review. The person seeking review shall have an opportunity to present 
evidence on his or her behalf in accordance with requirements established by the court. 
review the facts and circumstances that caused the police department to immobilize the 
vehicle or to tow and impound the vehicle, shall review any relevant documentation, and 
take into consideration the explanation provided by the owner on why the immobilization 
or impoundment was allegedly improper and issue his or her decision. Upon review, the 
parking administration manager may make any determination as necessary and proper to 
dispose of the case, including the release of the vehicle and the waiver of an applicable 
fines or fees. 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 3.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the subdivision described as The Bluffs, 12th Filing, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed as presented, and that 
the Mayor and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and directed to execute 
said plat, when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024.

PUDC-23-183: The Bluffs, 12th Filing (Expedited Review)
REQUEST:
Plat a portion of land in the NW Corner of Section 21, T.14N., R.66W., of 
the 6th P.M.
LOCATION:
East of Converse Avenue and south of Storey Blvd.
APPLICANT: 
Randy Guill
OWNER:

County A-1 and County LR
PURPOSE:

development and one remainder lot.

Connor White, Planner II
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Bluffs, 12th Filing Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body with staff conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
x 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for 29.61 acres of land. The subject property is 
unplatted, vacant land, which is concurrently being annexed to the City.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as developing with residential 
uses. The applicant has requested a future land use amendment for Lot 1, Block 1 to become 
Community Business. The plat provides larger lots than you would typically see for residential 
development unless used for multi-family or to be replatted at a later date. The plat does 
propose a commercial lot at a size that you would generally see.

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat divides the land into three large parcels and a small remainder parcel. Two of 
the large parcels are proposed to be for residential development and may be replatted in the 
future. The plat does connect Ogden Road to Storey Blvd. to the north and also proposes 
connecting Wild Bluff to Converse to the west.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$8,540 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$8,540 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$17,080.00 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee $1,100 per acre 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
The applicant proposes extending Wild Bluff to Converse and Ogden to Storey. These are 
major road connections that we would expect to happen with the proposed plat. Further 
road networks could be proposed with the replatting of Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, Block 3. 
There is a sliver of land that is between proposed Wild Bluff an Converse Avenue right-of-
way. The applicant will need to acquire an easement from the adjacent property owner 
prior to signing of the plat to make sure the connection will happen.    
 
Open Space (4.4): 
The applicant has requested that open space be addressed at a later date. The reasons they 
gave are as follows, Lot 1, Block 1 will have a site plan which will then address the open 
space requirement, Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, Block 3 will either be replatted in the future or 
they too will have site plans. Staff is satisfied with the applicants request as open space 
requirements will be required either with a site plan or when a replat is submitted.  
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. 

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any commercial building or 
residential building on any of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees 
are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the study provided. 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There is some follow-up information requested.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
This plat is proposing three buildable lots. Lot 1, Block 1 is proposed to be zone for commercial 
and the proposed lot size would be a buildable lot for that zone district. Lot 1, Block 2 and Lot 1, 
Block 3 are for future residential development. They could be used for multi-family at the 
proposed size if they were to get conditional use approval or they could be replatted into single-
family lots at a later date. 
 
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
Tract A is proposed to be a non-buildable lot. Note #5 on the plat indicates that it would be 
replatted in the future.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. The plat 
proposes two additional major street connections and three buildable lots. Two of the lots are 
proposed for future residential development which is similar to what is in the area as well.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Tract A is proposed to be a non-buildable lot. Note #5 on the plat indicates that it would be 
replatted in the future.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends minor 
corrections as conditions to the plat. These corrections should be resolved prior to final plat 
approval / signing of the mylars.  

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
   
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections (corrected with revised submittal). 
2. Some traffic concerns and road concerns (Partially corrected with revised submittal). 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant.  
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
 

PUDC-23-183 
 
 
January 2, 2024 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body with the following conditions: 
1. Prior to Governing Body approval: 
a. Updated traffic information be provided and approved by the Engineering Department. 
2. Prior to Mylars being signed: 
a. An easement across Townsend Place Apartments LP be recorded to allow Wild Bluff to 
connect into Converse Avenue. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-02-06_TotalExtraction_64219231.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION REQUESTING THAT THE WYOMING STATE 
LEGISLATURE CONSIDER LEGISLATION AUTHORIZING THE SALE 
OF ALCOHOLIC BEVERAGES WITH AN ALCOHOL BY VOLUME OF 
5% 
AT 
GROCERY 
STORES 
AND 
CONVENIENCE 
STORES 
THROUGHOUT THE STATE." 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body recognizes the need for improving the convenience of 
life for existing state residents, prospective new residents, and visitors alike;  
 
WHEREAS, one avenue for making daily life more efficient and convenient is to allow 
Wyoming residents and visitors to this great state to purchase alcoholic beverages with an alcohol 
by volume (ABV) of 5% at grocery stores and convenience stores, which stores can be easier to 
locate and more convenient to patronize than retail liquor stores; 
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body believes that the State's current statutory structure 
governing the sale of alcoholic beverages creates confusion on the part of Wyoming residents who 
cannot comprehend why alcoholic beverages may be sold at certain grocery stores and 
convenience stores in the state, but not at all of them; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body further believes that the current statutory structure 
governing the sale of alcoholic beverages is stifling economic growth in both larger and smaller 
communities because the number of liquor licenses that may be issued is tied to arbitrary 
population formulas specified by state statute; 
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body recognizes the value and necessity of local control 
regarding the issuance of liquor licenses by cities and towns, which are also charged with funding 
and administering emergency services and law enforcement;  
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body recognizes and appreciates the entrepreneurial spirit of 
the businesses who apply to the City of Cheyenne seeking a liquor license. However, the current 
statutory structure illogically constricts alcoholic beverage sales, placing governing bodies across 
the state in a precarious situation of picking “winners and losers” amongst a variety of qualified 
candidates. The Governing Body believes this is detrimental and antithetical to a free market 
economy; and 
 
WHEREAS the Governing Body has requested that the Wyoming Association of 
Municipalities draft a resolution on this topic similar to the one herein, which resolution, among 
other things, would also express support for grocery stores and convenience stores being able to 
sell alcoholic beverages with an ABV of 5%. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body hereby requests that the Wyoming State 
Legislature, and the appropriate legislative committees, review, consider, and adopt modifications 
to the current statutory structure governing the sale of alcoholic beverages in the state's city and 
1:04 pm, Feb 05, 2024

Page 2 of 2 
towns to allow alcoholic beverages with an ABV of 5% to be sold in grocery stores and 
convenience stores. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-02-06_TotalExtraction_10471589.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION REGARDING THE PROPOSED COWBOY SOLAR 
PROJECT, AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR TO PURSUE AND SECURE 
POSSIBLE IMPACT ASSISTANCE FUNDS ON BEHALF OF THE CITY 
AS PROVIDED BY WYOMING LAW." 
 
WHEREAS, on January 12, 2024, Enbridge Inc. submitted to the Wyoming Department of 
Environmental Quality, Industrial Siting Division, an Industrial Siting Permit Application in 
accordance with Chapter 12, Title 35 of Wyoming Statute to construct and operate an industrial 
facility, in the form of a solar energy and battery storage project, in unincorporated Laramie 
County (hereinafter referred to as "Cowboy Solar Project"); 
 
WHEREAS, a contested case hearing is currently scheduled to occur before the Industrial 
Siting Council March 25, 2024, through which the Council will review the socio-economic and 
environmental impacts of the industrial facility before issuing a permit for construction; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cowboy Solar Project will be constructed on private land in Laramie 
County approximately four (4) miles southeast of Cheyenne, Wyoming, east of U.S. Highway 85 
and both north and south of County Road 203. Regional access to the project site will be provided 
via U.S. Highway 85, and primary access will be provided by County Road 203; 
 
WHEREAS, the total area for the Cowboy Solar Project is approximately three thousand 
eight hundred forty-five (3,845) acres with the goal of the project to generate approximately seven 
hundred seventy-one (771) megawatts alternating current solar energy, paired with battery storage, 
and for the electricity to be used by large industrial corporate customers within Wyoming; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cowboy Solar Project will consist of a 400 megawatt alternating current 
(MWac) and a 371 MWac solar generation facility, respectively. Each solar generating facility will 
have a collection system, collection substation and battery energy storage system, each of which 
will include batteries composed of lithium iron phosphate; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cowboy Solar Project will consist of two (2) phases—"Cowboy Solar I" 
and "Cowboy Solar II," and construction period for the project is anticipated to begin in March of 
2025 and continue through August of 2027; 
 
WHEREAS, it is further anticipated that Cowboy Solar I will begin construction in March 
of 2025, will be ninety percent (90%) complete by October of 2026, and will be completed and 
fully operational by January of 2027. In turn, Cowboy Solar II construction is projected to begin 
in May of 2026, to be ninety percent (90%) complete by June of 2027, and be complete and fully 
operational by August of 2027; 
 
WHEREAS, local governments that will be primarily affected by the construction of 
industrial facilities are entitled to impact assistance payments for any unmitigated impacts caused 
by facility construction activities; and 
 
12:00 pm, Feb 06, 2024

Page 2 of 2 
WHEREAS, based on the geographic location of the site for the Cowboy Solar Project, it 
is anticipated that the City of Cheyenne, Laramie County and the Town of Pine Bluffs will be the 
local governments that will be primarily affected by the construction of this industrial facility, and 
thus, qualified to receive impact assistance payments. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Mayor is authorized to pursue and secure impact 
assistance payments on behalf of the City and to coordinate with the other local governments that 
will be primarily affected by the construction of the Cowboy Solar Project as the Industrial Siting 
Permit Application is being processed and reviewed by the Industrial Siting Division and 
ultimately considered by the Industrial Siting Council. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE CITY OF CHEYENNE TO 
APPROVE AND SUBMIT THE WYOMING DIVISION OF VICTIM 
SERVICES FUNDING APPLICATION FOR STATE FISCAL YEAR 2025 
AND 2026." 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 1-40-201 et seq, the Wyoming Victim and 
Witness Bill of Rights ensures that victims and witnesses of crime are treated with compassion, 
respect, and sensitivity within the criminal justice system; 
 
WHEREAS, the primary goal of the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance 
Program is to provide direct services to victims of crime after an incident is reported to law 
enforcement within city’s jurisdiction; 
 
WHEREAS, it is the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program’s mission 
to enhance the treatment of victims, witnesses, and survivors of criminal and non-criminal crisis 
situations by providing them with the assistance and services necessary to speed their physical and 
emotional recovery; to support and aid them through the process of the criminal justice system; 
and to make referrals to appropriate agencies; 
 
WHEREAS, services provided by the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance 
Program include the following; crisis intervention, emotional support, emergency assistance and 
shelter options, information and referrals to community resources, case status disposition, criminal 
justice advocacy and, assistance with offering and completing Crime Victim Compensation 
Program applications for the State of Wyoming Division of Victim Services; 
 
WHEREAS, during the last fiscal year, of the individuals served by the Cheyenne Police 
Department Victim Assistance Program, nine hundred twenty-one (921) victims of violent crime 
were provided with one-on-one advocacy support. This support included crime scene crisis 
response, hospital forensic examination accompaniment, emotional support, connection to 
community and mental health resources, assistance with funeral preparations, liaison assistance 
with City and County Prosecuting Attorneys’ offices, education about the criminal legal system, 
physical safety plan, coping skills and mental health safety planning, suicidality assessments and 
intervention, assistance with Crime Victim Compensation, short term financial stability assistance, 
intervention with landlords and employers, and referrals to specialized community support 
agencies; 
 
WHEREAS, the services provided by the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance 
Program have been crucial to the long-term stabilization and well-being of the city’s community 
members; particularly people with lower financial stability and those with vulnerabilities, and they 
would not be possible without the funding and staffing necessary to provide those services and 
resources; 
 
WHEREAS, the services of the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program 
are funded in part by state and federal grants established by the Victims of Crime Act ("VOCA"), 
11:48 am, Feb 07 2024

Page 2 of 3 
the Violence Against Women Act ("VAWA"), and the Family Violence Prevention and Services 
Act ("FVSPA"); 
 
WHEREAS, these state and federal grants are awarded through one, or a combination of, 
State Domestic Violence and Sexual Assault funds, State Victim/Witness funds, State Child 
Advocacy funds, State Salary funds, Federal Violence Against Women Act ("VAWA") funds, 
Sexual Assault (SASP) funds, Federal Victim of Crime Act (VOCA) funds and Federal Family 
Violence Prevention Act (FVPSA) funds; 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 1-40-118, award recipients of these state and 
federal funds are required to submit subgrantee reports to the Wyoming Division of Victim 
Services ("DVS") in accordance with deadlines established through the Office on Violence Against 
Women and the Family Violence Prevention and Services Program; 
 
WHEREAS, to further support its services, the City of Cheyenne Police Department 
Victim Assistance Program intends to apply for these funds to provide basic, direct services to 
victims of crime in compliance with the Wyoming Division of Victim Services Rules; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Police Department Victims Assistance 
Program intends to apply for these funds through the WyoGrants System by Friday, March 15, 
2024, for State Fiscal Years 2025 and 2026; and 
 
WHEREAS, funding awarded through the State Division of Victim Services will be 
utilized by the Cheyenne Police Department Victim Assistance Program to cover approximately 
fifty percent (50%) of the annual employment costs for salary and benefits for one (1) full-time 
Victim Assistance Coordinator and two (2) full-time Victim Advocates.  Funding from these state 
and federal grants will also be utilized to cover costs of cellular phones for staff members, office 
supplies, training expenses, costs associated with educational and contact brochures for the 
department, casework tracking software, and emergency assistance funding to assist crime victims 
with immediate safety, housing, and medical access concerns. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body approves the funding application and 
application process through the Wyoming Division of Victim Services State Fiscal Year 2025 and 
2026 (July 1, 2025, to June 30, 2026). 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Mayor and the City Clerk are authorized to execute 
all documents relating to the Wyoming Division of Victim Services State Fiscal Year 2025 and 
2026 (July 1, 2025, to June 30, 2026). 
 
 
 
 
 
 

Page 3 of 3 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-02-06_TotalExtraction_88542229.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Governing Body, City of Cheyenne 
FROM: 
 
Renee Smith, Grants Manager 
DATE: 
 
FEBRUARY 16, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE EPA CLIMATE POLLUTION REDUCTION 
PLANNING GRANT 
 
The City of Cheyenne has been awarded a U.S. Environmental Protection Agency Climate Pollution Reduction 
Grant for planning in the amount of $1,000,000. Grant funds will support staffing and consultant services to 
develop a Priority Climate Action Plan (PCAP), a Greenhouse Gas Inventory, and a Comprehensive Climate 
Action Plan to support emission reductions, renewable energy initiatives, and other energy efficiencies for 
Cheyenne consumers. The CPRG Planning Grant and published PCAP allows the City of Cheyenne to pursue 
an EPA CPRG Implementation Grant for eligible projects, such as community solar. 
This is formula grant through the Inflation Reduction Act of 2022 and the performance period of the grant 
award is 01/01/2024 to 06/30/2027.  
Please let me know if you have specific questions about the CPRG program and this grant opportunity.  
Respectfully, 
Renee Smith 
(307) 637-6285 
rsmith@cheyennecity.org 
 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A 
RESOLUTION 
APPROVING 
THE 
ENVIRONMENTAL 
PROTECTION 
AGENCY 
CLIMATE 
POLLUTION 
REDUCTION 
PLANNING GRANT IN THE AMOUNT OF ONE MILLION DOLLARS 
TO 
PURSUE 
GREENHOUSE 
GAS 
REDUCTION 
PLANS 
AND 
INITIATIVES SUCH AS A COMMUNITY SOLAR PROJECT." 
 
WHEREAS, the Environmental Protection Agency (EPA)'s Climate Pollution Reduction 
Grants (CPRG) program provides a total of five billion dollars ($5,000,000,000) in grant funding 
to states, local governments, tribes, and territories to develop and implement ambitious plans for 
reducing greenhouse gas emissions and other harmful air pollution; 
 
WHEREAS, Phase 1 of the CPRG program provides flexible support to states, local 
governments, tribes, and territories regardless of the status of their climate planning and 
implementation process; 
 
WHEREAS, CPRG planning grant recipients can utilize funding to design climate action 
plans that incorporate a variety of measures to reduce Priority Climate Action Plan (PCAP) and 
Greenhouse (GHG) emissions from across six key sectors including electricity generation, 
industry, transportation, buildings, agriculture/natural and working lands, and waste 
management; 
 
WHEREAS, as a condition of the one-million-dollar ($1,000,000) Phase 1 CPRG 
program planning grant, the City of Cheyenne must submit a PCAP to the EPA by March 1, 
2024; 
 
WHEREAS, within two (2) years after receiving a Phase 1 CPRG planning grant, the 
City of Cheyenne must submit a Comprehensive Climate Action Plan (CCAP) and within four 
(4) years after receipt, the City must submit a Status Report; 
 
WHEREAS, upon submitting a CPRG planning grant to develop its PCAP, the City of 
Cheyenne will be eligible to compete for a CPRG implementation grant in an amount between 
two million dollars ($2,000,000) and five hundred million dollars ($500,000,000) per individual 
grant; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne has identified investigating the 
feasibility of incorporating renewable energy systems, including a community solar project, as a 
City Council goal; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne is interested in better understanding its GHG 
emissions and wishes to support efforts to stimulate innovative technologies and practices that 
create cost saving and more sustainable efficiencies for Cheyenne residents to meet growing 
utility needs. 
 
 
1:00 pm, Feb 16 2024

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne is hereby 
designated as the authorized representative of the City to act on behalf of the Governing Body on 
all matters relating to the one-million-dollar ($1,000,000) CPRG planning grant and is authorized 
to execute the grant agreement and to submit the PCAP, CCAP and Status Report, as wells as 
any other associated documents. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED this ___ day of __________, 2024. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

NOTICE OF AWARD
Based on your Application dated 12/28/2023 including all modifications and amendments, the United States acting by and through the US Environmental
Protection Agency (EPA) hereby awards $ 1,000,000.00. EPA agrees to cost-share 100.00% of all approved budget period costs incurred, up to and not
exceeding total federal funding of $ 1,000,000.00. Recipient's signature is not required on this agreement. The recipient demonstrates its commitment to carry
out this award by either: 1) drawing down funds within 21 days after the EPA award or amendment mailing date; or 2) not filing a notice of disagreement with
the award terms and conditions within 21 days after the EPA award or amendment mailing date. If the recipient disagrees with the terms and conditions
specified in this award, the authorized representative of the recipient must furnish a notice of disagreement to the EPA Award Official within 21 days after the
EPA award or amendment mailing date. In case of disagreement, and until the disagreement is resolved, the recipient should not draw down on the funds
provided by this award/amendment, and any costs incurred by the recipient are at its own risk. This agreement is subject to applicable EPA regulatory and
statutory provisions, all terms and conditions of this agreement and any attachments.

Attachment 1 - Project Description
The agreement provides funding under the Inflation Reduction Act (IRA) to the City of Cheyenne,
Wyoming to develop a comprehensive, economy-wide climate mitigation plan or update an existing plan
in collaboration with air pollution control districts, large and small municipalities statewide, and tribal
governments that will support actions to reduce greenhouse gases (GHG) and harmful air pollutants and
to conduct meaningful engagement with low-income and disadvantaged communities. The City of
Cheyenne is leading the development and implementation of innovative strategies to reduce GHG
emissions. The Cheyenne Metropolitan Statistical Area (MSA) has experienced an increase in flooding
and has been affected by forest fires, which are both attributed to shifting weather patterns. In 2020, over
176,000 acres were burned in the Mullen Fire, threatening the city’s watershed, releasing large quantities
of GHG, and causing significant harm to numerous ecosystems. To address these challenges, the City of
Cheyenne will lead the efforts to address the MSA’s most pressing environmental difficulties, while also
fostering positive economic development through innovative solutions and workforce development
opportunities. Power generation, transportation, manufacturing, industrial, and renewables are the
primary sectors to be addressed in the mitigation plans.In general, activities include the development,
updating, and evaluation of plans to reduce climate pollution (i.e., to reduce GHG emissions and/or
enhance carbon sinks). Specifically, the City of Cheyenne will create a comprehensive GHG emission
inventory to develop GHG reduction measures, identify strategies to implement energy efficiency, and
conduct outreach programs to enhance public awareness and participation.Three key deliverables will be
produced and submitted over the course of the four-year program period, including: a Priority Climate
Action Plan (PCAP), due March 1, 2024; a Comprehensive Climate Action Plan (CCAP), due two years
from the date of the award; and a Status Report, due at the close of the grant period.
Expected outcomes include a PCAP and CCAP that identifies: tons of pollution (GHGs and co-pollutants)
reduced over the lifetime of the measures; tons of pollution (GHGs and co-pollutants) reduced annually;
and tons of pollution (GHGs and co-pollutants) reduced with respect to low-income and disadvantaged
communities. Other expected outcomes include: improved ambient air quality and public health;
increased public awareness; improved staff capacity; development of a City of Cheyenne Standing
Committee on Sustainability; implementation of energy efficiency and other improvements to reduce
operating costs; and positive economic development impacts.
The intended beneficiaries include all residents and visitors of the Cheyenne MSA, with a specific focus
on low income and disadvantaged communities to promote environmental equity. The City of Cheyenne
will proactively seek public engagement with the low income and Hispanic populations to strengthen
relationships that invite dialogue and spark innovation. The City of Cheyenne will take active measures to
engage these communities to increase involvement and create a comprehensive benefits analysis.City of
Cheyenne anticipates $15,000 in subawards, $5,000 per year for years 1-3. Subawards will support and
enhance project goals and deliverables and facilitate public outreach efforts. Cheyenne anticipates 1-2
subawards per year.

5D - 00I10301 - 0      Page 5
Administrative Conditions
National Administrative Terms and Conditions
General Terms and Conditions
The recipient agrees to comply with the current EPA general terms and conditions available at: https:
//www.epa.gov/grants/epa-general-terms-and-conditions-effective-october-1-2023-or-later.
These terms and conditions are in addition to the assurances and certifications made as a part of the
award and the terms, conditions, or restrictions cited throughout the award.
The EPA repository for the general terms and conditions by year can be found at: https://www.epa.
gov/grants/grant-terms-and-conditions#general.
A.  Correspondence Condition
The terms and conditions of this agreement require the submittal of reports, specific requests for
approval, or notifications to EPA.  Unless otherwise noted, all such correspondence should be sent to the
following email addresses:
• Federal Financial Reports (SF-425):  rtpfc-grants@epa.gov and Grant Specialist, Jennifer Hale
at hale.jennifer@epa.gov. 
• MBE/WBE reports (EPA Form 5700-52A): Grant Specialist, Jennifer Hale at hale.jennifer@epa.
gov. 
• All other forms/certifications/assurances, Indirect Cost Rate Agreements, Requests for
Extensions of the Budget and Project Period, Amendment Requests, Requests for other Prior
Approvals, updates to recipient information (including email addresses, changes in contact
information or changes in authorized representatives) and other notifications: Grant Specialist,
Jennifer Hale at hale.jennifer@epa.gov. 
• Quality Assurance documents, workplan revisions, equipment lists, programmatic reports and
deliverables: Project Officer, Emily Bertram at Bertram.Emily@epa.gov.
B.   Pre-Award Costs
In accordance with 2 CFR 1500.9, the recipient may charge otherwise allowable pre-award costs (both
Federal and non-Federal matching shares) incurred from Janurary 1, 2024 to the actual award date
provided that such costs were contained in the approved application and all costs are incurred within the
approved budget period.

5D - 00I10301 - 0      Page 6
Programmatic Conditions
Climate Pollution Reduction Grants Specific Programmatic Terms and Conditions
A.  PERFORMANCE REPORTING AND FINAL PERFORMANCE REPORT 
Performance Reports – Content
In accordance with 2 CFR 200.329, the recipient agrees to submit performance reports that include brief information on each of the following
areas:  1) A comparison of actual accomplishments to the outputs/outcomes established in the assistance agreement work plan for the
period; 2) The reasons why established outputs/outcomes were not met; and 3) Additional pertinent information, including, when
appropriate, analysis and explanation of cost overruns or high-unit costs.
Additionally, the recipient agrees to inform EPA as soon as problems, delays, or adverse conditions which will materially impair the ability to
meet the outputs/outcomes specified in the assistance agreement work plan are known.
Performance Reports - Frequency
Quarterly performance reports are required to be submitted electronically to the EPA Project Officer within 30 calendar days after the
reporting period (every three-month period). Quarterly reports are due according to the following schedule. If a due date falls on a weekend
or holiday, the report will be due on the next business day. If a project start date falls within a defined reporting period, the recipient must
report for that period by the given due date unless otherwise noted. This quarterly reporting schedule shall be repeated for the duration of
the award agreement.
July 1 – September 30 Reporting Period: report due date October 30 (note, in year 1, this reporting period should begin at the project start
date)
October 1 – December 31 Reporting Period: report due date January 30
January 1 – March 31 Reporting Period: report due date April 30
April 1 – June 30 Reporting Period: report due date July 30
The recipient must submit the final performance report no later than 120 calendar days after the end date of the period of performance.
Subaward Performance Reporting
The recipient must report on its subaward monitoring activities under 2 CFR 200.332(d). Examples of items that must be reported if the
pass-through entity has the information available are:
1. Summaries of results of reviews of financial and programmatic reports.
2. Summaries of findings from site visits and/or desk reviews to ensure effective subrecipient performance.
3. Environmental results the subrecipient achieved.
4. Summaries of audit findings and related pass-through entity management decisions.
5. Actions the pass-through entity has taken to correct deficiencies such as those specified at 2 CFR 200.332(e), 2 CFR 200.208 and the 2
CFR Part 200.339 Remedies for Noncompliance.
B.  DELIVERABLES AND REQUIREMENTS
Municipalities and Air Pollution Control Agencies that accept an award are required to produce and electronically submit the following three
deliverables to EPA by the dates specified:
1.) By March 1, 2024, a Priority Climate Action Plan (PCAP), which is a narrative report that includes a focused list of near-term, high-
priority, implementation ready measures to reduce Greenhouse Gas (GHG) pollution and an analysis of GHG emissions reductions that

5D - 00I10301 - 0      Page 7
would be achieved through implementation. These initial plans can focus on a specific sector or selected sectors, and do not need to
comprehensively address all sources of GHG emissions and sinks in the jurisdiction. The PCAP must include: a GHG inventory; quantified
GHG reduction measures; a low-income and disadvantaged communities benefits analysis; and a review of authority to implement.
2.) A Comprehensive Climate Action Plan (CCAP), due 2 years from the date of the award. The CCAP is a narrative report that should touch
on all significant GHG sources/sinks and sectors present in a state or metropolitan area, establish near-term and long-term GHG emission
reduction goals, and provide strategies and identify measures to achieve those goals. Each CCAP must include: a GHG inventory; GHG
emissions projections; GHG reduction targets; quantified GHG reduction measures; a benefits analysis for the full geographic scope and
population covered by the plan; a low-income and disadvantaged communities benefits analysis; a review of authority to implement; a plan
to leverage other federal funding; and a workforce planning analysis.
3.) A Status Report, due at the closeout of the 4-year grant period. This report should include the implementation status of the quantified
GHG reduction measures included in the CCAP; any relevant updated analyses or projections supporting CCAP implementation; and next
steps and future budget/staffing needs to continue CCAP implementation.
Climate plans for metropolitan areas should also be developed with regional coordination as much as possible, and applicants are
encouraged to coordinate with geographically proximate tribes as appropriate. In all cases, the lead organization for a state or metropolitan
area PCAP funded through the CPRG program must make the PCAP available to other entities by March 1, 2024 for their use in developing
an implementation grant application.
MSA lead organizations must involve stakeholder groups and the public in the process for developing the PCAP and CCAP. Potential
stakeholders include urban, rural, and underserved or disadvantaged communities as well as the general public, governmental entities,
federally recognized tribes, Port Authorities, labor organizations, community and faith-based organizations, and private sector and industry
representatives.
C.   Cybersecurity Condition 
Cybersecurity Grant Condition for Other Recipients, Including Intertribal Consortia
(a) The recipient agrees that when collecting and managing environmental data under this assistance agreement, it will protect the data by
following all applicable State or Tribal law cybersecurity requirements.
(b) (1) EPA must ensure that any connections between the recipient's network or information system and EPA networks used by the
recipient to transfer data under this agreement, are secure. For purposes of this Section, a connection is defined as a dedicated persistent
interface between an Agency IT system and an external IT system for the purpose of transferring information. Transitory, user-controlled
connections such as website browsing are excluded from this definition.
If the recipient's connections as defined above do not go through the Environmental Information Exchange Network or EPA's Central Data
Exchange, the recipient agrees to contact the EPA Project Officer (PO) no later than 90 days after the date of this award and work with the
designated Regional/Headquarters Information Security Officer to ensure that the connections meet EPA security requirements, including
entering into Interconnection Service Agreements as appropriate. This condition does not apply to manual entry of data by the recipient into
systems operated and used by EPA's regulatory programs for the submission of reporting and/or compliance data.
(2) The recipient agrees that any subawards it makes under this agreement will require the subrecipient to comply with the requirements in
(b)(1) if the subrecipient's network or information system is connected to EPA networks to transfer data to the Agency using systems other
than the Environmental Information Exchange Network or EPA's Central Data Exchange. The recipient will be in compliance with this
condition: by including this requirement in subaward agreements; and during subrecipient monitoring deemed necessary by the recipient
under 2 CFR 200.332(d), by inquiring whether the subrecipient has contacted the EPA Project Officer. Nothing in this condition requires the
recipient to contact the EPA Project Officer on behalf of a subrecipient or to be involved in the negotiation of an Interconnection Service
Agreement between the subrecipient and EPA.
D.  Competency Policy
Competency of Organizations Generating Environmental Measurement Data
In accordance with Agency Policy Directive Number FEM-2012-02, Policy to Assure the Competency of Organizations Generating
Environmental Measurement Data under Agency-Funded Assistance Agreements, recipient agrees, by entering into this agreement, that it
has demonstrated competency prior to award, or alternatively, where a pre-award demonstration of competency is not practicable, the
recipient agrees to demonstrate competency prior to carrying out any activities under the award involving environmental information
operations (i.e., the collection, production, evaluation or use of environmental information and/or the design, construction, operation, or
application of environmental technology). The recipient shall maintain competency for the duration of the agreement's period of
performance, and this will be documented during the annual reporting process.  To access the Policy and other information about the Policy,

5D - 00I10301 - 0      Page 8
visit http://www.epa.gov/fem/lab_comp.htm. A copy of the Policy is available directly at https://www.epa.gov/measurements-
modeling/documents-about-measurement-competency-under-assistance-agreements, or a copy may be requested by contacting the EPA
Project Officer for this award.
E.  Public or Media Events
The Recipient agrees to notify the EPA Project Officer listed in this award document of public or media events related to activities
accomplished as a result of this agreement, and provide the opportunity for attendance and participation by federal representatives with at
least ten (10) working days' notice.
F.  Geospatial Data Standards
All geospatial data created must be consistent with Federal Geographic Data Committee (FGDC) endorsed standards.  Information on these
standards may be found at https://www.fgdc.gov/.
G.  Quality Assurance 
A Quality Assurance Project Plan (QAPP) is a record of determinations made during planning by the organization of the data type,
quality, and quantity necessary to meet project objectives and the quality assurance and quality control procedures, specifications,
and documentation that the recipient will use to ensure the desired results. This quality assurance documentation requirement
applies to all grants that involve environmental information operations (i.e., the collection, production, evaluation or use of
environmental information and/or the design, construction, operation, or application of environmental technology).
1.
To be approvable, the grant recipient must develop a QAPP or equivalent QA document that meets all requirements outlined in
EPA QA Project Plan Requirements, QA/R-5. The term QAPP will be used hence forward in this grant QA Term and Condition
(R8 QA T&C) to refer to the QAPP or equivalent QA document.
2.
As applicable and in accordance with 2 CFR 1500.12, the recipient agrees to document in a QAPP, the quality assurance (QA),
quality control (QC), and technical activities that must be implemented to ensure that the project objectives are met. Multiple
QAPPs and/or a multi-year QAPPs may be prepared as directed in the Work Plan and by the EPA Project Officer.
3.
Recipients implementing environmental information operations (defined in Section 1) within the scope of the assistance
agreement must submit a QAPP to the EPA Project Officer and EPA QA reviewer for review and approval no later than 90 days
prior to performing work that includes environmental information operations. Earlier submissions are welcome.
4.
The QAPP shall be prepared for each project in accordance with (IAW) EPA QA/R-5: EPA Requirements for Quality Assurance
Project Plans. For each QAPP, complete the Region 8 QA Document Review Crosswalk (QAPP Review Crosswalk). In the QAPP
Review Crosswalk the recipient shall identify the specific page number(s) and section(s) of the QAPP that addresses the
corresponding required QAPP element in QA/R-5, including a detailed explanation for any elements considered not applicable to
the grant. The recipient shall submit the completed crosswalk with the QAPP to the EPA Project Officer and EPA QA contact for
review and approval. Each QAPP has a maximum period of performance of 5 years.
5.
The recipient agrees to ensure that no environmental information operations (defined in Section 1) occur without a QAPP that is
approved by the EPA Regional Quality Assurance Manager or their delegate, except under circumstances requiring immediate
action to protect human health and the environment or operations conducted under police powers. In the case of this exception,
QAPPs must be approved within 30 days of the recipient's first date responding to the incident.
6.
For multi-year QAPPs, the recipient must review the current QAPP annually to reconfirm its suitability and effectiveness and its
alignment with the grant's work plan. The recipient shall document the review using the Region 8 QA Document Review
Crosswalk (QAPP Review Crosswalk). In the QAPP Review Crosswalk the recipient shall identify the specific page number(s) and
section(s) of changes to the approved version of the QAPP and include a description of what changed and why in the crosswalk
element's comment box. For multi-year QAPPs, the documented annual QAPP review results (using the QAPP Review
Crosswalk) must be submitted to EPA Project Officer and QA reviewer annually, no later than 60 days prior to the anniversary
date of EPA's approval of the QAPP as a new document. Significant changes to the project require the recipient to modify the
QAPP and submit the updated QAPP as a new document as described in Sections 4-5. The recipient must submit the updated
QAPP and completed Region 8 QA Document Review Crosswalk to the EPA Project Officer and QA reviewer for review and
approval. Only after the revision has been approved shall the change be implemented.
7.
H.  Use of Logos
If the EPA logo is appearing along with logos from other participating entities on websites, outreach materials, or reports, it must not be
prominently displayed to imply that any of the recipient or subrecipient's activities are being conducted by the EPA. Instead, the EPA logo
should be accompanied with a statement indicating that City of Cheyenne received financial support from the EPA under an Assistance
Agreement. More information is available at: https://www.epa.gov/stylebook/using-epa-seal-and-logo#policy.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-02-06_TotalExtraction_66849318.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE SUPPORT OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY FOR CITY STAFF TO REVIEW AND REWRITE THE CITY'S 
PARKING ORDINANCES FOR THE PURPOSE OF INTRODUCING PAID 
ON-STREET PARKING IN DOWNTOWN CHEYENNE AND DIRECTING 
CITY STAFF TO SUBMIT THE ORDINANCE REWRITE FOR 
GOVERNING BODY CONSIDERATION." 
 
WHEREAS, in October of 2023, through Resolution No. 6359, the Governing Body 
directed the Cheyenne Police Department to conduct a study exploring the transition to a 
professional services model for downtown parking. The resolution described the current parking 
program as "vertically integrated" and questioned whether the Police Department had "sufficient 
resources to administer the downtown parking program without some form of additional 
assistance[;]" 
 
WHEREAS, Resolution No. 6359 also asserted that "transitioning to a contract or a 
professional services model should bring unrealized efficiencies to parking downtown and to the 
downtown parking program, notably, through enhanced enforcement resources, which may assist 
the City in better collecting fines imposed for parking violations." The Resolution further stated 
that "[r]egrettably, the collection of parking ticket fines has proved challenging with the 
outstanding balance of unpaid parking tickets for fiscal year 2023 equaling approximately two 
hundred fifty thousand dollars ($250,000.00) [;]" 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Police Department completed the required study and submitted 
its findings to the Governing Body on December 11, 2023. The study explored multiple private 
parking companies, included reviews of several municipalities' approaches to parking regulation 
from the region and nation, and included interviews with parking managers, directors and 
consultants; 
 
WHEREAS, of the private parking firms contacted, LAZ Parking was the only company 
to provide an estimate of cost. LAZ Parking is a parking management, transportation and mobility 
company. LAZ Parking recommended a parking program that was nearly identical to the City's 
current program, but with lower-paid employees and significantly higher administrative costs. This 
led the Police Department to conclude that the current parking program model offers the citizens 
of Cheyenne a better service that is not profit-driven and which is at a lower cost than that available 
through private contracts; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne currently collects parking ticket fines from ninety-one 
percent (91%) of tickets issued, which is substantially better than the six (6) other communities 
evaluated in the study. Of note, in the proposal provided from LAZ Parking, the company promised 
a seventy percent (70%) fine collection rate; 
 
WHEREAS, based on the Cheyenne Police Department's continued research of parking 
best practices and how the City's parking program may be improved, the Department made several 
recommendations, many of which are already being implemented; 
11:34 am, Feb 21, 2024

Page 2 of 3 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Police Department has begun working in conjunction with the 
City Attorney's Office and the Governing Body to make the parking ordinances clearer and more 
concise while ensuring due process protections are maintained for the benefit of the general public. 
Furthermore, the Department is requesting funds in the next fiscal year's budget to transfer one of 
the Department's part-time parking enforcement positions to a full-time position. In addition, the 
Department's parking administration manager has secured membership with the National Parking 
Association, a national trade association that promotes and supports parking industry research, 
education and advocacy. Through this membership, the administration manager is taking 
advantage of contacts and resources to help better evaluate employee job descriptions, titles, 
uniforms, and vehicle decals as well as developing an established best practices training program; 
 
WHEREAS, the Mayor of the City of Cheyenne and Director of the Community Recreation 
and Events Department have already worked to hire a private security firm, Signal Security, to 
provide overnight security patrols at the Jack Spiker Parking Structure. This initiative has already 
proven to be an extremely effective addition to the already over 1,100 patrols conducted by the 
Cheyenne Police Department in the garage annually. The Community Recreation and Events 
Director has also researched and received several quotes for security access gates to be installed 
at the parking structure and is working with Passport, which provides the City's parking 
management software, to ensure any additions of access gates are compatible with the current 
parking software. In the time since the study required under Resolution No. 6359, the Cheyenne 
Police Department has also begun community engagement regarding downtown parking by 
speaking with a downtown business owner group and registering for an upcoming town hall. The 
Department will continue to seek out other such engagement opportunities; 
 
WHEREAS, several other items of concern were noted in the study, such as "block face" 
parking language in City ordinance being confusing, and a lack of other options for businesses that 
would benefit from a broader range of parking plans other than "two-hour." Other concerns 
centered on an inability to efficiently track parking behavior and downtown traffic patterns, and to 
address other modern parking concerns due to the rigidity of the current two-hour parking 
regulations. All of these issues could be addressed by taking the advice recently provided to the 
neighboring cities of Fort Collins and Loveland by Walker Consulting of providing paid on-street 
parking; 
 
WHEREAS, paid on-street parking has been supported by the majority of attendees at a 
recent meeting of downtown business owners attended by the Cheyenne Police Department. In 
fact, in a recent survey of 102 downtown stakeholders, more respondents either supported or 
strongly supported the proposal of paid on-street parking than did not support such a proposal; and 
 
WHEREAS, a paid on-street parking program, where pricing and zoning are established 
by governing body resolution, would provide flexibility on parking options for businesses and 
consumers while still encouraging parking turnover by providing less expensive options in the 
parking garages. An app-based paid on-street parking program will help drive downtown economic 
growth by creating more parking turnover and incentivizing more voluntary compliance with City 
parking regulations. 
 

Page 3 of 3 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that City staff are directed to conduct a review and re-write of the 
City of Cheyenne parking ordinances to allow for the use of app-based paid on-street parking to 
be presented to the governing body for consideration at a later date; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the ordinance changes required in accordance with 
this resolution shall provide for the establishment of pricing and zoning by governing body 
resolution, taking into consideration relevant parking data, community input, and industry best 
practices. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-02-06_TotalExtraction_58137625.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE SUPPORT OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY FOR AN ACTION PLAN TO ADDRESS DEFICIENCIES 
REGARDING SIDEWALKS IN THE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT 
DISTRICT." 
 
WHEREAS, the Downtown Development District ("DDA") is the heart of the 
community and the center of tourism for the City of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, public safety is a primary concern in the DDA District;  
 
WHEREAS, sidewalks in poor repair decrease the overall appeal of the DDA District; 
 
WHEREAS, property owners owe a general duty to maintain their property, including 
adjacent sidewalks, in a reasonably safe condition; 
 
WHEREAS, it is the legal responsibility of the property owner to maintain sidewalks 
adjacent to their property by Cheyenne City Code 12.04.120.B; 
 
WHEREAS, the Mayor’s Council for People with Disabilities has expressed concern 
regarding the condition of sidewalks throughout the DDA District; 
 
WHEREAS, in light of these concerns, in 2014, the DDA Board commissioned a report 
from Benchmark Engineering; and 
 
WHEREAS, the 2014 Benchmark Engineering Report contains recommendations that 
remain valid today. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that all city departments shall be responsible for identifying 
sidewalk problems within the DDA District and shall coordinate a program for the identification, 
notification, and citation of trip hazards on sidewalks; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that ADA guidelines shall be taken into consideration 
and that the recommendations contained in the 2014 Benchmark Engineering Report shall be 
reviewed;  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the DDA Capital Improvement Grant (CIG) funding 
along with the City’s miscellaneous concrete program funding shall be considered to assist DDA 
property owners in funding these sidewalk repairs; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the work of coordination by the city departments be 
completed by March 25, 2024. 
 
 
11:54 am, Feb 21 2024

PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk
